Your three images don't line up with the Blender scene well enough to submit, so I used them as look references and rendered three matching stills from the actual Blender camera. Nothing has been uploaded, submitted or spent.

## Your references against the Blender move

- **Three different rooms:** each image is its own building (different ceilings, shelving and doorway), so they can't describe one environment.
- **Layout conflicts:** two have display islands in the aisle where our camera travels, and the third puts six cases on floor pedestals in a round room. Our six cases float into a flat row.
- **Invented content:** they are full of made-up game artwork, lettering and figures, plus what looks like a classic arcade alien icon. Seedance would reproduce and garble all of that.
- **What I took from them:** the violet-magenta neon rectangles on the ceiling, teal light on the shelves, a golden doorway with warm light at the foot of the walls, a glossy tiled floor with strong reflections, glass cases with lit edges, and neon rings in the chamber.

The Blender stills are sparser and calmer than your images: one case per bay plus a small upper shelf, no plants, screens or merchandise piles. Say if you want it denser.

## What changed in Blender

The corridor is now veined stone niches with brushed-metal shelves, glass-fronted cases, a bronze doorway and a chamber with side walls, rendered with Cycles.

- **Preserved:** camera path and hero cases are the same code. Final card positions moved 0 px and the background colour is identical, on both desktop and phone. The only last-frame differences are slight smoothing on cover lettering edges (under 1% of pixels).
- **Render time:** 75 to 125 seconds per corridor frame at full quality, 5 to 45 in the chamber. I estimate roughly two hours for the full desktop sequence; that is not rendered.
- **Site:** the frames on the site are still the blockout's.

## Seedance 2.5 configuration (prepared only)

| Setting | Value |
|---|---|
| Mode | References (`omni_reference`) |
| Duration, size | 5 s, 720p, 16:9 |
| Audio, bitrate | off, standard |
| Video reference | `1-motion-reference.mp4`: the whole move in the vault look |
| Start frame | `2-start-frame-opening.png`: frame 0 of the move |
| Image references | `3-look-mid-corridor.png`, `4-look-doorway.png` |
| Cost | 35 credits (15 at 480p, 80 at 1080p); balance 881 |

- **Pinning the opening frame:** allowed together with a video reference, and the price is unchanged. The setting is defined as the first frame of the output, so it targets the first test's main failure, the skipped start.
- **Stills are frames of the move itself** (0, 36 and 67 of 120), so video and images show one place from one camera.
- **Covers have their lettering removed** rather than being flat colours.

## Prompt

> Follow the reference video exactly: it is the camera move, the timing and the layout of this shot, frame for frame. One continuous, slow, steady forward glide down the centre of a long corridor, then through a tall rectangular doorway framed in golden light into a wider dark chamber, where six upright panels turn to face the camera and settle into one level row. The first frame of the shot is the start image: begin there and start moving at once, at the reference video's pace. Reach the doorway when the reference video does. No cuts, no speed changes, no camera shake, no added camera moves.
>
> Keep the room shown in the reference video and images: the same walls, niches, shelves, display cases, ceiling lights and doorway, in the same places and the same number. It is one place from start to end. Do not add rooms, furniture, plants, signs, screens or objects.
>
> Make it photographic. The walls are dark honed stone with fine pale veins. Shelves, case frames and ledges are dark brushed metal with soft streaked highlights. Every display case has a real pane of glass over its picture, with faint reflections that slide across it as the camera passes. The floor is polished dark stone tiles with thin joints; it reflects the neon and the cases softly, sharper near the camera and fading with distance. Rectangles of violet-magenta neon tube hang under the ceiling. Teal light runs along the front edge of each shelf and washes the stone beneath it. A thin warm golden light frames the doorway and spills a little onto the floor. Deep violet ambient light, true black in the shadows, soft contact shadows where shelves meet walls and cases meet shelves. A faint haze softens the far end of the corridor. Light sources glow gently; nothing blooms or flares.
>
> The pictures in the cases are abstract soft colour shapes only, and stay as they are: no text, no letters, no numbers, no logos, no symbols, no characters, no faces, no game artwork, no user interface. No people. Nothing spins, drifts off or bursts; no particles, sparks or lens flares.
>
> The chamber beyond the doorway has a flat, evenly lit violet back wall and two rings of neon under its ceiling. The six panels end evenly spaced in one straight row, upright and square to the camera, in front of that violet wall.

The test ran once for exactly 35 credits (balance 881 to 846). It is much better than the first test: Seedance followed the Blender camera from the first frame to the last. It did not make the scene more realistic, though, so I would still use Blender's own frames for the corridor.

## The five points

| Point | Result |
|---|---|
| Opening from frame 0 | Yes. The first frame is the Blender first frame. |
| Camera path | Followed. Once reframed, the picture sits within 2 px of the reference across the frame. |
| Doorway timing | On time, with no lag in any second. |
| Materials and lighting | No gain in realism. Lighting and colour are kept, but fine detail is about half the reference's and small covers go soft. |
| Six-panel handoff | Six panels, in order, on the right violet. They end 3.6 px from Blender's card positions but wander up to 20 px while settling, so not exact enough for the handoff. |

## A fixed framing offset

Seedance's clip comes out about 2% smaller than the reference, anchored at the top-left corner, identically in every frame; the first test had it too. One constant scale-and-crop removes it, and the "reframed" figures use that copy. As delivered, the picture sits 6 to 16 px off.

## Against the first test

| | First test | Second test |
|---|---|---|
| Starts at the first frame | no, one second in | yes |
| Doorway | 0.4 s late | on time |
| Outline match with the reference | 0.17 | 0.49 as delivered, 0.88 reframed |
| Same room throughout | no | yes |
| Chamber colour | black | the page's violet |
| More realistic than the reference | no | no |

The difference came from the motion video and stills showing the same store, and from pinning the first frame.

## Is it good enough for the corridor?

On camera fidelity, yes. I would still not use it, for three reasons:

- **It adds nothing visually:** it is the Blender look, slightly softer.
- **Format:** it is 720p and 16:9 only. The site needs 1440×810 frames and a phone layout, and this model has no 9:16.
- **Blender's frames** line up with the handoff by construction and cost no credits.

What the test proved is that Seedance keeps a camera move when the references agree and the first frame is pinned. A more photographic corridor would need appearance stills that are themselves photographic, which is a third test and your call.

## Other notes

- **One stutter:** around half a second in, one frame repeats and catches up two frames later.
- **Nothing invented:** no text, logos, people or objects were added, and the aisle stayed empty.
- **No flicker:** it matches the reference.
- **Measurement limit:** camera position is inferred from the picture, not recovered in 3D.

The three photographic stills are rendered, and they pass on consistency but not on realism: they read as a good render of the store, not as photographs. On your own rule, I would not spend another 35 credits on them. Nothing was submitted or spent.

## What I could and couldn't do

I have no image generator in this session other than Higgsfield's, which costs credits, so I couldn't produce AI-repainted versions myself. I pushed the Blender scene as far toward a photograph as it goes, from the same three camera frames:

- **Printed game cases:** covers are now glossy prints lit by white strip lights under each shelf, not glowing pictures. The boxed games have a coloured strip across the top and their own plastic.
- **Imperfections:** every small case stands slightly off true, and the glass carries smudges.
- **Camera behaviour:** shallow depth of field, highlights that roll off as on film, lens glow, darker corners and fine grain.

## Evaluation

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Same architecture, camera angle and doorway | Yes. Outlines match the current Blender frames at 0.88 to 0.91 with no shift. |
| Aisle clear | Yes, in the two corridor frames. |
| One continuous store | Yes. The three share colours as closely as the current frames do; they are one scene. |
| Convincingly photorealistic | No. |

Why they fall short of photographs:

- **The covers are still illustrations.** A real shelf carries photographic or painted box art with fine print; ours are flat drawings, and that reads as CG at once.
- **The shelving is too clean.** There are no price rails, hooks, dividers, wear or dust.
- **The neon lost colour.** The film-style rendering turns the violet tubes nearly white and dulls the gold doorway, and the chamber's back wall drifts from violet toward blue.

## Options

- **Keep Blender as the final animation.** This is what your rule points to, and it needs no more credits.
- **Repaint the three frames outside.** I prepared a kit: the three frames, a distance map of each as a structure guide, one shared prompt and a must-not-appear list. If you repaint them and put the results in a folder, I can measure whether they kept the layout, the empty aisle and one consistent palette before anything goes to Seedance. The risk is the one your first references had: three separately repainted images tend to drift into three different stores.

The frames for the site are untouched: the photographic option is off unless asked for, and card positions still match the blockout to 0 px. It is committed on the local `cinematic-prototype` branch (`01226ce`), and the checkout is back on `main`. Nothing is merged, pushed or deployed.

## Findings

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| 1. Camera angle and perspective | Mid-corridor and doorway are close. The opening is not: Blender's frame 0 looks down the aisle from the left with the far wall about 31% across the frame; your opening is nearly centred, with it about 40% across. |
| 2. Shelves and architecture | Same kind of store: large glass cases on a shelf, rows of boxes below, shelves above, neon rectangles, track spotlights, gold doorway. But there are more, narrower bays (about 9 or 10 large cases a side against Blender's 6) and more ceiling rectangles. |
| 3. Same store across the three | Yes. Shelving, floor, lighting and palette agree, more closely than our own three frames do. The artwork on a given case changes from image to image. |
| 4. Aisle and doorway | The aisle is empty in all three. The doorway image keeps the gold frame, the ceiling rings and the back wall in the same place; its gold frame is slightly narrower, with shop shelving added outside it. |
| 5. Six-card handoff | Six panels in roughly the right places, but they are framed paintings of landscapes hung on the walls, not our floating product covers. Blender has to supply the final section regardless. |
| 6. Photorealism | Significantly better: real materials, glass reflections, physical boxes, a believable floor. |

Outline agreement with the Blender frames is low (0.06 opening, 0.21 mid, 0.22 doorway), so these are re-compositions of the store, not repaints of our frames.

## What Seedance may struggle with

- **The opening.** Test 2 worked because the pinned first frame was the video's first frame. Pinning this opening asks it to start centred while the video starts off to the left, which risks a lurch or a skipped start, as in test 1.
- **Two looks again.** The motion video is the illustrated store and the images are photographic. In test 2 the two agreed completely; here the model has to take motion from one and appearance from the other, and I can't predict which wins.
- **Bay count.** More, narrower bays in the images than in the video may make shelves and cases swim or merge as the camera moves.
- **Changing artwork.** Covers differ between the images and the video, so they are likely to morph during the move.
- **Small lettering.** The small boxes carry tiny label-like marks and corner badges. They're illegible, but Seedance tends to turn such marks into visible gibberish.
- **Shelving beside the doorway** in the third image is not in the video at that point.

## Recommended inputs and settings

Same structure as test 2:

| Setting | Value |
|---|---|
| Mode | Seedance 2.5, References |
| Duration, size | 5 s, 720p, 16:9, audio off, standard bitrate |
| Video reference | the store-look motion reference from test 2 |
| Start frame | the opening image, once it matches frame 0 |
| Image references | your mid-corridor and doorway images |
| Cost | 35 credits (15 at 480p, 80 at 1080p); balance 846 |

The prompt would say the video gives camera and layout only and the images give the look, and forbid text on the packaging. The estimate was read live just now with stand-ins for the files; I'd re-check it on the real uploads and stop above 35.

If you would rather test with the images as they are, I'd still pin the opening, since an unpinned start is what failed in test 1, and expect the first second to be the weak part.

The audit is done and nothing on the branch was changed. Most of the integration already exists; the real remaining work is rendering the store-look sequences and making frame loading lazier, since the final frames are about three times heavier than the blockout.

## Already complete

- **Homepage integration:** the Top Deals block is the real backend-driven React block at all times; the animation is a canvas behind it.
- **Six-card handoff:** case positions come from Blender, and the page fades straight into the cards when they match within 1 px and dissolves when they don't. The store look keeps those positions to 0 px on both layouts.
- **Scrolling:** forward and reverse scrubbing with smoothing, and a memory budget for decoded frames (48 MB desktop, 24 MB phone).
- **Layout shift:** the section's space is reserved before any frame loads; it measured zero shift.
- **Fallbacks:** reduced motion, data saving, slow connection, low-memory device, unsupported browser and tablets all get the ordinary block with no frames downloaded.
- **Baseline:** 158 frontend tests pass, and lint and the type check are clean. The animation code is 5.6 KB and loads only where it will run.

## Remaining work

1. **Build script:** the publishing path only knows the blockout look. It needs the store look, and it should resume a long render where it stopped.
2. **Render the final sequences:** about 1 h 25 min for desktop and 1 h 05 min for phone on this Mac.
3. **Lazier loading:** today every frame starts downloading as soon as the homepage opens, even if the visitor never scrolls to the block. It should fetch the starter set only when the block is near, and the rest as the visitor approaches.
4. **Caching:** frames are served with revalidation on every visit. The versioned folder should get a long-lived cache header in the Vercel config.
5. **Hardening:** a missing frame currently comes back as the app's HTML page with a success status; the loader should check it got an image.
6. **Tests:** only the placement arithmetic is unit-tested. The fallback decision and the frame loader need tests.
7. **Browser checks:** re-measure smoothness, memory and alignment with the real frames at 1024, 1280, 1440 and phone width, and test Safari, which has never been checked.
8. **Docs:** `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` doesn't mention this feature or its place in the scope table.
9. **Backend tests:** these need Docker, which is not running.

## Expected asset sizes

Measured on production-quality sample frames, then scaled to the full sequences:

| | Desktop (120 frames, 1440×810) | Phone (96 frames, 720×1280) |
|---|---|---|
| Corridor frame | about 103 KB | about 57 KB |
| Chamber frame | about 16 KB | about 9 KB |
| Whole sequence | about 8.1 MB | about 3.6 MB |
| Needed before it can start | about 1.1 MB | about 0.45 MB |
| Blockout today, for comparison | 2.7 MB | 1.2 MB |

These are WebP at quality 75. Quality 60 saves about 15%. AVIF came out only about 5% smaller than that, so I wouldn't add a second format.

## Performance risks

- **Download weight:** 3.6 MB on a phone is significant on mobile data. Lazy loading is the main mitigation, with quality 60 as the fallback.
- **Decode cost:** detailed frames take longer to decode than flat ones, so the earlier smoothness figure has to be re-measured. Memory use should not change, since frame dimensions are the same.
- **Stale covers:** the frames are rendered with today's six deals. If the deals change in the backend, the animation ends on the old covers and dissolves into the new cards until the frames are re-rendered.
- **1024 px windows** still dissolve, because the cases sit 4 px off the cards there.
- **Repository size** grows by about 12 MB of frames.

## Decisions for you

- **Branch base:** this branch sits on `design-refresh`, which you haven't adopted on `main` yet. Shipping the animation means shipping that design pass too.
- **Old blockout frames:** I'd remove them from the site once the store frames are in, so 4 MB of unused files don't ship.

Everything stays on the `cinematic-prototype` branch, with no merge or deployment.

The proposed grade is ready for review. It brings the corridor much closer to your reference: neutral dark walls and ceiling, deeper blacks and about a third less saturation, with the neon and gold still bright. No production assets were changed.

One thing the grade cannot do: carry the dark look into the last frame. That frame has to stay exactly as rendered, because its background is the page's own violet and its six cases are the real covers. The grade is therefore full for the first 78% of the move and eases off over the last stretch, with the last few frames untouched.

## What the grade does

| Your request | How it is handled |
|---|---|
| Deeper, richer blacks | Tones are matched to the reference's spread of light and dark; the darkest 2% of the picture drops to about half its level. |
| Restrained purple and magenta | Walls, ceiling and floor that were bathed in violet go dark and near neutral. Overall saturation falls from 0.56 to 0.36; your reference measures 0.36. |
| Less exaggerated cyan and teal | Teal keeps about 60% of its colour. |
| Highlight control | Only dim violet is reduced. Bright violet, the neon itself, is left alone, so the tubes glow against dark surfaces. |
| Natural contrast, not underexposed | Average brightness is unchanged (0.240 before, 0.244 after). |
| Warm gold preserved | Red through gold keeps at least 92% of its colour. |
| Floor reflections | Still present, with the violet and teal in them toned down. |

Unlike your reference, the grade cannot add real materials, glass or re-lit surfaces. It changes colour and tone only, so the result is a darker, calmer version of the illustrated store.

## Consistency between frames

The grade is one fixed colour rule that never looks at the frame it is applied to, so it cannot change from one frame to the next. Checked on all 120 draft frames:

- **Largest brightness step between neighbouring frames:** 0.033 graded, against 0.031 in the original. That step is the scene itself, at the doorway.
- **Brightness step added by the grade alone:** at most 0.006 per frame.
- **Easing stretch (frames 93 to 118):** saturation rises gradually from 0.56 to 0.81, at most 0.027 per frame, with brightness nearly flat. It reads as the chamber's lights coming up as the cases settle.
- **Last frame:** identical to the render on both desktop and phone.

## The decision in the chamber

The chamber's back wall is the page's violet, which the grade turns dark grey-violet before easing it back. If you would rather the dark look hold all the way to the cards, the page's band colour behind Top Deals would have to change to match. That is a site design change, so I have not touched it.

## Process

- **Reusable:** the rule is stored as a small settings file plus a standard colour table, and one command applies it to any folder of frames, desktop or phone.
- **No re-render:** it runs on already-rendered frames in seconds.
- **Git:** the tool and the proposed rule are committed on the local `cinematic-prototype` branch (`381b4d6`). Nothing is merged or deployed.

The review material is ready, and I recommend easing **B**, which holds the full dark grade until the six cases have landed and then releases it over the last 17 frames.

One thing shapes the comparison: the camera passes through the doorway at about frame 76 of 120 (63% of the move). The current easing only starts at frame 93, so it already holds the dark look well past the doorway. That means "retained longer" and "as we enter the chamber" pull in opposite directions, so I made both.

## The three easings

| | Full grade until | Released by | What you see |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A · Current** | frame 93 | frame 117 | The chamber starts brightening while the cases are still gliding in. |
| **B · Held to the landing** | frame 102 | frame 119 | Everything that moves is in the dark look; the chamber's light comes up once the cases have landed. |
| **C · Released at the doorway** | frame 74 | frame 102 | The violet returns as the camera passes through the doorway; the dark look is gone soon after entering. |

Why B:
- **No colour shift during movement:** the release happens entirely in the stretch where the picture is already still (frames 103 to 119).
- **It leads into the handoff:** the room reaches the page's violet on the last frame, just before the real cards take over.

The catch with B is that it is the quickest release: saturation changes by up to 0.033 per frame, against 0.027 for A and 0.021 for C. The scene's own largest step, at the doorway, is 0.030. If it feels abrupt in the clip, starting it a few frames earlier softens it.

## Checks

- **Blender, not Seedance:** every clip is built from the 120 draft frames Blender rendered. No Seedance output is in any of them.
- **Last frame:** byte-identical to the render in all three easings. Its back wall measures (52, 18, 129), which is the page's `#341281`.
- **Six-card handoff:** the grade changes colour only, so the case positions are unchanged.
- **Brightness:** it moves by at most 0.004 per frame in the last 30% in all three; the release is almost entirely a change in colour.
- **Top Deals background:** not touched, and no site files or published frames were changed.

## Limits of this review

- The motion clips are draft-quality frames at 1280×720, and the six cases in them carry stand-in covers.
- The phone is shown as stills only, because only sample frames exist for it. The easing is defined as a share of the move, so it applies to the phone's 96 frames the same way.
- The grade tool now takes the easing as two numbers, committed on the local `cinematic-prototype` branch (`763ced6`). Its default is still A until you choose.

## Cover quality

The files are crops of pictures of display cases, not flat artwork, which causes three problems:
- **Clipped titles:** Minecraft, Call of Duty and Cyberpunk 2077 lose their last letter at the right edge; Fortnite and FC 25 touch it.
- **Baked-in case edges:** each had a strip of frame or purple backdrop on the left, which I trimmed off. A small glass glint remains in the top-left corner of most.
- **GTA VI:** the logo in the bottom-right corner is cut off.

They are readable at panel size, but clean flat covers at 600×800 with the title fully inside would fix all three.

These covers also carry publishers' logos and a real player's name, whereas the site's current covers are its own drawings. That is worth a thought before a public deployment.

## Backend check

- **The cards use other pictures:** every product card shows the site's own cover from `/covers/<game>.svg`, so even the three matching titles would change picture at the handoff.

## What the transition does

- **Cases settle first:** the six cases come to rest exactly over the cards' cover positions, as before.
- **A band of light crosses the row, left to right:** ahead of it you see the cases; behind it, the real Top deals cards with their real names, prices and discounts. The two blend only inside the light, in a strip about half a card wide.
- **Driven by scroll position alone:** scrolling back up runs it in reverse.
- **Cards are clickable only once the light has fully passed.**
- **Data:** the cards come from the live backend through a read-only local stand-in. Shadow Protocol is not in the catalogue and the Top deals ranking is untouched.

| Page errors | none | none |

## What the audit found

- **Video 2 is the earlier recording** `design-review/cinematic/harness/scroll-1440.mp4` (byte-identical to your attachment).
- **The handoff code never changed.** In video 2 the cases carried the same six products as Top deals, in the same order, with each product's own cover picture. The page saw that and used its seamless path: cases settle onto the cards, then the details fade in around covers that do not change.
- **What changed was the content.** Your six covers were different pictures in a different order from the cards, so the page fell back to its mismatch path. That was a dissolve, which I then replaced with the light sweep.
- **Now:** the sweep is removed. The site's handoff code is identical to what video 2 was recorded with, apart from one line that lets a local frame set be chosen.

## The tradeoff

For the artwork to be continuous with accurate product data, two things must both hold:

1. **Same products, same order.** Each case must stand for the product whose price appears under it. Top deals is currently Minecraft, Hogwarts Legacy, EA Sports FC 25, God of War, DOOM Eternal, Forza Horizon 5.
   - Shadow Protocol cannot be in the row: it is not a product, and under its cover would sit Forza Horizon 5's price.
   - God of War is in the row and you supplied no cover for it, so it carries the site's own drawn cover. It is the odd one out visually.
   - DOOM Eternal and Forza Horizon 5 move to 5th and 6th.
2. **Same picture on the card.** The card's cover has to be the picture the case carries. So your five covers become those five games' cover pictures on the site, replacing the drawn ones.

The preview shows exactly this. The local stand-in only swaps the picture path for those five games; names, prices, discounts, stock and order pass through from the live backend untouched. Because the swap is by game, those five show your covers on every page of the local preview, not only in Top deals.

To ship it, the five pictures have to reach the real cards, and there are two ways:
- **Point the five games' cover path at the new files:** a small catalogue data change, which needs your approval.
- **Keep the paths and replace the files under the same names:** no backend change, but each file grows from about 1 KB to 100–200 KB.

Top deals is recomputed from discounts. When it changes, the chamber frames need re-rendering for the new row; until then the page falls back to the dissolve.

| Page errors | none | none |

- **Cover continuity:** each cover changes by about 5 to 6 of 255 per colour value between the cases alone and the finished cards (2 for the drawn God of War cover). In a close crop the covers look the same before and after; the preview frames are draft renders saved as JPEG.
- **Details:** the title, discount strips, platform, name and price fade in over the last stretch of scroll and become clickable when mostly visible.
- **Frontend checks:** 158 tests pass, with type check, lint and production build clean. Backend tests were not run because Docker is not running, and Safari is still untested.
- **Preview limits:** the desktop frames are a 6-minute draft render of the last 60% of the move, joined to the earlier draft for the first 40%. The phone preview starts 40% in. The phone row is Minecraft and Hogwarts Legacy.

Buy Now works through the existing cart. It is committed on the local cinematic-prototype branch; nothing is merged, pushed or deployed.

The Top deals cards are rebuilt in React and CSS to match your reference, and the six cases still land on the card covers and stay there while the details fade in beneath them. It runs in the local preview at `http://localhost:5173/`. No new Blender sequence was needed.

## What the inspection found

- **No conflict from the taller card.** The handoff only depends on the cover's box. The new card keeps it exactly: a one-pixel border, the cover edge to edge, 3:4. The stage measures where the covers are, so the extra height below them does not matter.
- **Square cases against rounded covers.** The cases are rectangles, so their corners would have poked out around the rounded cards. The stage now reads the cards' rounding and rounds the cases' corners to match, gradually, once they have landed.
- **Buy Now needs a seller's offer, and a card only knows the best price.** Buy Now therefore asks for the product (the same request the product page makes), puts its best in-stock offer in the cart and opens the cart. If nothing can be bought or the request fails, it opens the product page instead.
- **Two older problems surfaced and are fixed:**
  - At 1024 px the cases sat 4 px off, so that width fell back to the dissolve. The row's gaps now scale with its width, and it lands within 0.47 px.
  - Colour-graded frames have a darker wall than the page, which would have shown as strips around the frame while it settles. The stage now fills with each frame's own wall colour.

## What was built

- **Card:** dark gradient, rounded corners, thin lit violet border with a soft glow, large cover with rounded top corners, bold white title, purple discount badge, large white price with the struck-through original, purple Buy Now button with a cart icon.
- **Hover:** the border brightens and the card lifts 2 px; with reduced motion there is no lift and no transition.
- **Data:** names, discounts, prices, stock and offer counts come from the backend, and Top deals is still calculated there. The title is the product's name without its platform and region, which sit on a small line beneath; screen readers get the full name.
- **Covers:** the five approved covers are used for those games on these cards; God of War keeps its current placeholder.
- **Section background:** stays the page's violet, because the last frame has to match it. Your reference is darker; changing it would mean new frames.

## Test results

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Cover-to-card alignment | 0.01 px at 1280 and 1440, 0.47 px at 1024, 0 px on the phone; all seamless |
| Forward, reverse, very slow, fast flick | 16.7 ms average per frame; none over 33 ms |
| Same picture reached scrolling down and up | identical at three positions, desktop and phone |
| Layout shift when frames arrive late | 0 at 1024, 1280, 1440 and phone |
| Horizontal overflow | none on desktop or phone |
| Reduced motion, data saving, 2g, low memory, no frames | ordinary block shown, no frames downloaded |
| Buy Now, against the local backend in Docker | the card's best offer is in the cart at the card's price, and the cart is priced by the server (desktop and phone) |
| Frontend | 164 tests pass; type check, lint and build clean |
| Backend | 278 tests pass; style check clean |

- **Phone:** two columns; the first two covers take the handoff and the other four cards follow below.
- **Layout shift at 1440:** the page grew 56 px lower down while loading on the slowed connection; the deals section and the block after it did not move.
- **Checkout itself** was not exercised beyond the cart: it needs a sign-in.
- **Timings** are headless Chrome on this Mac, and Safari is still untested.

## Things to know

- **Painted covers are on the Top deals cards only.** The store, search and product pages still show the drawn covers for those five games.

## How it works

- **No frames fade or swap.** The last frame of the video stays on screen. Once the camera has stopped, the "Top deals" heading and a short hint fade in over it.
- **Hover a panel:** its outline lights up and a details card appears with the product's name, discount, price, original price and number of offers. All of it comes from the backend; nothing is read from the picture.
- **Add to cart** puts the product's best offer in the cart and stays in the showroom, with the button showing "Added ✓". **Buy Now** does the same and opens the cart.
- **Without a mouse:** pressing a panel pins its details open, the keyboard reaches each panel, and Escape closes it.
- **Matching products to panels:** each panel is tied to a game, not a position, so the backend can reorder Top deals freely. If the block ever holds a product that has no panel, the page shows the ordinary cards instead of the showroom.

| Same picture reached scrolling down and up | identical at three positions |

- **One fix on the way:** the project's own pre-deploy check allows exactly one headers rule in the hosting config, and the frame cache header I had added broke it. I removed the header rather than loosen the check. Frames are therefore revalidated by the browser on repeat visits instead of being cached for a year.

|---|---|

| Keyboard, Add to cart, Buy Now | pass; the cart is priced by the live server |
| Same picture scrolling down and up | identical at three positions |
| Scroll timings | 16.7 ms average per frame; none over 33 ms |
| Phone and reduced motion | ordinary cards; Buy Now from a phone card reaches the cart |
| Secrets audit before each push | passes |

## One thing to check first

Your section 4 describes six floating covers settling onto the Top Deals cards. The live section is the Kling showroom you chose later: the last frame stays and the six panels are the cards. I left that exactly as it is, and the existing showroom checks pass unchanged.

## What the page does now

- **Hero:** the artwork slides down slightly inside the banner and grows by up to 12% from the foot of the tree, so it moves more slowly than the text. The headline, description and button rise and fade only as they leave the window.
- **Best sellers:** each card starts 32px low, leaning 9° back and transparent, then stands up into place, left to right, with its text following its picture. Near the top of the window it shrinks a little and dims.
- **Into the store:** the page around the stage dims to the store's darkness over the last three quarters of a window, so the store rises into an already dark page. It clears again when the stage leaves.
- **Ambient:** the dimming is the only ambient effect. I added no particles, sweeps or glow overlays.

Everything follows scroll position alone, so it reverses exactly. Nothing is pinned or hijacked, and none of it runs with reduced motion. There is no animation library; the shared code is in `web/src/motion/`.

Two things you will notice:
- On a laptop-height window the Best sellers row is already partly in view at load, so cards 4–6 sit part-way through arriving until the first scroll.
- The "entering" feeling comes from the dimming and the receding cards only. The camera still starts moving when the stage pins; starting it earlier would change the sequence, so I did not.

## Results

Measured in headless Chrome on the built site at 1024, 1280, 1440, and phones at 375 and 390:

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Sideways overflow | 0px at all five |
| Layout shift while loading and scrolling | 0 at all five |
| Same picture scrolling down and back up | identical on desktop; on phones the effects' state is identical and under 0.03% of pixels differ at some stops, including one where no effect is active |
| Cards clickable while arriving, in place and receding | all, and a click opens the product |
| Reduced motion | nothing moved, dimmed or pinned |
| Frame timing: full run down and up, slow, fast with direction changes | 16.7 ms average; worst single frame 33 ms, also with the processor slowed four times |
| Against the previous build | no measurable difference at 1440 or on the phone |
| Store frame loader and memory | unchanged: same 105 frames, at most 10 decoded, none held off screen |
| Tests | backend 278 passed, frontend 193 passed, lint, type check and secrets audit pass |

The main bundle grew by about 1.5 kB gzipped, and no extra images or video are downloaded.

## What you should know

- **A check was already failing:** the cover check in `make test` has flagged the `art` picture folder since those pictures were added, including at the last deploy, when I reported the web checks as passing. I fixed the check to look only at drawn covers.
- **Hover lift:** cards now animate their small lift on hover. The transition was naming the wrong property, and I had to change it for the reveal.
- **Stalls in early runs:** two early runs each had a single half-second stall, like the one on the live site before this work. None appeared in the final runs or the comparison.
- **Not tested:** Safari and real phones.

> - Recap shown in the app: Slow scrolling through the store now glides between frames instead of stepping, committed locally on cinematic-prototype (bacd3c1). All tests and browser checks pass. It is not deployed, and it doubles the section's frame download from 8.2 MB to 16.3 MB.

Slow scrolling through the store now glides from frame to frame instead of stepping. It is committed locally on `cinematic-prototype` (`bacd3c1`) and not deployed; the live site still has the stepping version.

## Why it stepped

The store is a sequence of still frames. The live set kept every second frame of the video, so the picture held still for about 18 pixels of scrolling and then jumped.

## What changed

- **Twice the frames:** every frame of the video is kept while the camera moves, 209 instead of 105, which halves each jump.
- **Blending between frames:** between two frames the next one is drawn over the current one, more solid the further you scroll, so the picture passes from one into the next.
- **Sharp at rest:** two blended frames look like motion blur while moving but soft when still, so once you stop scrolling the picture eases onto the nearest whole frame.

## Measured

On the same very slow scroll (300 pixels at 40 pixels a second, 1440 wide):

| | Live version | New version |
|---|---|---|
| Times the picture changed | 17 | 133 of 136 pictures drawn |
| Typical size of each change | 33 | 5.5 |
| Picture at rest | one whole frame | one whole frame |

The side-by-side recording I sent shows it; the file is `design-review/smooth-frames/very-slow-before-left-after-right.mp4`.

Nothing else regressed:
- **Showroom:** hover details on all six panels at 1024, 1440 and 1920, keyboard, Add to cart and Buy Now all pass, with the same picture scrolling down and back up.
- **Timing:** no frame over 33 ms at 1440, 1024 or the phone, including with the processor slowed four times.
- **Tests:** backend 278 passed, frontend 200 passed.

## The cost

The section's frames grow from 8.2 MB to 16.3 MB for desktop visitors who scroll to it. Lower picture quality barely helped (14 MB at a visibly lower setting), so I kept the quality.

- The download before the section can start is unchanged at 1.1 MB.
- The rest now arrives evenly spread, so halfway through it is already as fine as the live set.
- Decoded pictures held in memory are unchanged.

If 16 MB is too much, the alternative is blending on the existing 8.2 MB set. It is smooth too, but the panels nearest the camera show as two offset copies while scrolling slowly.

## Two things I could not settle

- A tiny layout shift (0.008) was recorded once during loading at 1440 in two of three runs. It did not appear when I instrumented it, so I have not identified what moved.
- This is measured in headless Chrome only; I have not seen it on your screen or in Safari.

## Response 69

> - Ran a command: Read how the backend is hosted and time it while warm
> - Loaded tools: select:WebSearch
> - Ran a command: Read the recorded cold start measurement
> - WebSearch: Render free web service spin down after 15 minutes inactivity cold start instance hours 2026
> - WebSearch: Render Starter instance price per month vs Railway Hobby plan price 2026
> - Recap shown in the app: Nothing was changed. The backend answers in about 0.3 seconds when awake; the wait you saw is the free hosting plan putting it to sleep after 15 minutes without visitors. Moving hosts is not needed to fix that.

No, I would not move it. The host is not slow; the backend is asleep when you arrive, and that can be fixed where it is.

## What is actually happening

- **Awake, the backend is fast.** I timed it just now: 0.2 to 0.3 seconds per request.
- **The free plan puts it to sleep.** Render stops a free service after 15 minutes with no traffic and restarts it on the next request. We measured that wake-up at 25 seconds on 17 November; Render says it can take about a minute, which is what the notice on the site tells visitors.
- **The frontend (Vercel) and database (Neon) are not the cause.** The page frame appears at once; only the data waits.

Another free host would behave the same way, since free plans that run a Docker container also sleep or ask for a card. So moving costs work and risk right before submission without solving the cause.

## Options

| Option | Cost | Effect | Catch |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Scheduled ping (my recommendation)** | Free | The backend rarely sleeps | Not a guarantee: GitHub can run schedules late |
| Render's Starter plan | $7 a month | Never sleeps | Needs a payment card; your decision |
| Move to Railway | About $5 a month, plus usage | Never sleeps | Paid as well, and a full migration of config, docs and checks |

**The ping:** a small scheduled job in this repository (GitHub Actions) that requests the health route every 10 minutes. It needs no new account. One always-on service fits inside Render's 750 free hours a month (a 31-day month is 744). Two caveats: GitHub pauses scheduled jobs after 60 days without repository activity, and it is a standing job, so I need your go-ahead to add it.

If you want certainty while Eneba is reviewing, the $7 plan for one month is the only option that guarantees it; you can switch back afterwards.

Sources:
- [Deploy for Free – Render docs](https://render.com/docs/free)
- [Render pricing explained (livemy.app)](https://livemy.app/blog/render-pricing)
- [Render vs Railway 2026 (kunalganglani.com)](https://www.kunalganglani.com/blog/render-vs-railway-2026.md)
- [Railway vs Render pricing (costbench.com)](https://www.costbench.com/compare/railway-vs-render/)

Yes, and that is still the right picture: the site is in three parts, and only one of them sleeps.

| Part | Host | Sleeps? |
|---|---|---|
| The pages you see (React app) | Vercel | No, served at once |
| The API that supplies products, cart, sign-in, orders (Laravel) | Render, free plan | Yes, after 15 minutes without traffic |
| The database | Neon | Not the cause of the wait |

When you open the site, Vercel shows the page frame immediately, then the page asks the API on Render for the products. If Render is asleep, that request is what takes up to a minute. Vercel cannot run the Laravel API itself, which is why it lives on Render.

So the monthly plan goes on **Render**, not Vercel. Vercel stays free.

## Before you switch the plan

The repository's Render config (`render.yaml`) still says `plan: free`, and Render re-applies that file on every push to `main`. If you pick the paid plan in the dashboard and leave the file as it is, the next push would likely put the service back on free. Render's docs say conflicting dashboard changes are overwritten on sync; I did not find that stated for the plan field specifically.

I would remove the plan line from the file first. Render then keeps whatever plan the service has, so the dashboard decides and pushing the change cannot start any billing by itself.

## What you do in Render

1. Open the `eneba-marketplace-api` service in the Render dashboard.
2. Add a payment method under Billing. Type the card there yourself; never paste it into this chat.
3. In the service's settings, change the instance type from Free to Starter ($7 a month).
4. Leave the workspace plan on the free one. Only the service's instance type needs to change.

I cannot see your dashboard, so the labels may differ slightly. Check the price shown before you confirm.

Yes. The slow first load is gone: the backend no longer goes to sleep, so the site's data should arrive in well under a second every time you open it, instead of taking 25 seconds to a minute after it had been idle.

The picture caching part only takes effect on Vercel, so it is not verified until deployed. The idle test on the paid plan was spoiled by my own measurements.

I found the cause and have a fix ready, but it is not deployed yet. The site was telling browsers not to keep any file, so every time a page was shown again its pictures were requested from the server again.

## What I measured on the live site

In a real headless Chrome at 1440 wide, leaving a page and pressing Back:

| Coming back to | Page redrawn in | Requested again from the server |
|---|---|---|
| Homepage, from a product | about 50 ms | 138 showroom frames and the homepage data |
| Homepage, from the Store | about 30 ms | 194 showroom frames and the homepage data |
| Store list, from a product | about 40 ms | 11 covers and the product list; 7 of 12 visible covers were blank for 100 ms |

So the page itself returns quickly, with its scroll position. What makes it feel like a reload is the pictures being fetched again. On a slower connection than my test machine, the blank covers last longer.

## The fix

- **Browsers may now keep the site's files.** Built scripts and styles are kept for good, showroom frames for 30 days, covers for an hour. Pages and API answers are still requested every time.
- **The app keeps page data longer.** Data for a page you have left is kept 30 minutes instead of 5, so returning later shows the page at once instead of grey placeholders.
- **Lists are not refetched within a minute.** This covers the homepage, the store and the Game Archive. A product page and the cart are still requested every time.

Two consequences to know:

- A replaced cover can take up to an hour to show for someone who already saw the old one.
- A price or stock level on a card can be up to a minute old. The server still prices every order itself.

## Checks

- On a local build, returning to the homepage or store within a minute made no list request at all.
- The secrets audit, all 279 frontend tests and the deploy config check pass. I extended the config check so it fails if a page or an API path is ever given a cache lifetime.
- **Not verified:** the picture caching. It is set in Vercel's config and only takes effect on Vercel, so I can confirm it only after deploying, by re-running the same measurement.

## Two corrections to earlier work

- **My first measurement was wrong and I discarded it.** The in-app browser pane was hidden, which freezes page animations, and it showed a one-second delay on Back that does not exist in a real browser.
- **The idle test on the paid plan is spoiled.** My measurements here hit the backend during its 17 quiet minutes, so the result will not prove it stays awake. Render's page showing the $7 plan remains the evidence; I can rerun the test once we stop touching the site.

The fix is on a local branch, `faster-returns` (`e4257b2`), built on what is live now.

```text
## Follow-up: deployment configuration facts (Render free Docker web service + Neon Free), read 2026-11-16

Method caveat as before: pages were read through a summarising fetch tool; short quotes are reliable. YAML example blocks on Render's Blueprint spec page did not come through at all (collapsed), so exact example syntax there is unverified. No files written.

---

### 1. Render Docker web services

**Port contract** — https://render.com/docs/web-services
- "Every Render web service must bind to a port on host `0.0.0.0` to serve HTTP requests."
- "We recommend binding your HTTP server to the port defined by the `PORT` environment variable."
- "The default value of `PORT` is `10000` for all Render web services." It can be overridden by setting `PORT` as an environment variable on the service.
- "If you bind your HTTP server to a different port, Render is usually able to detect and use it." If no bound port is detected, the deploy fails.
- "Render forwards inbound traffic to only one HTTP port per web service." Reserved ports: 18012, 18013, 19099.
- EXPOSE: unconfirmed. Neither the web-services nor the Docker page mentions it. https://render.com/docs/multi-service-architecture tells you to indicate the HTTP port in the Dockerfile with a link to the EXPOSE instruction, while an old community staff answer (search snippet only) says EXPOSE is not used and ports are detected from the running process. Safe contract: listen on `0.0.0.0:$PORT` (10000 by default); do not rely on EXPOSE.

**Health checks** — https://render.com/docs/health-checks
- Set via dashboard or the Blueprint `healthCheckPath` field; path must start with `/`.
- 2xx or 3xx within five seconds = healthy; 4xx/5xx = unhealthy.
- During a deploy: if the new instance does not pass within 15 minutes, Render cancels the deploy and keeps routing to the existing instance.
- Running service: 15 seconds of consecutive failures -> Render "temporarily stops routing traffic to it"; 60 seconds -> "automatically restarts the instance."
- Unconfirmed: whether health-check requests behave differently on Free, or whether they count as inbound traffic that keeps a free service awake (page says nothing).

**Zero downtime** — https://render.com/docs/deploys, https://render.com/docs/docker
- "All service types redeploy with zero downtime, unless they attach a persistent disk." Docker page: "Your Docker-based services support zero-downtime deploys".
- If any command fails or times out, the deploy fails and the previous deploy keeps running. Timeouts: build 120 min, pre-deploy 30 min, start 15 min.
- Unconfirmed for Free specifically: no page states an exception, but none confirms it either (and a spun-down free service has no running instance anyway).

**Multi-stage target** — https://render.com/docs/docker
- "Render supports parallelized multi-stage builds." No setting for choosing a target stage is documented, and the Blueprint spec has no target field among the Docker fields (only `dockerfilePath`, `dockerContext`, `dockerCommand`). A 2021 community thread (snippet only) says target selection is not supported. Treat as not available: the last stage of the Dockerfile must be the runtime image.

---

### 2. render.yaml (Blueprint) — https://render.com/docs/blueprint-spec

Field names and values read today:
- `type`: `web` (also `pserv`, `worker`, `cron`, `keyvalue`, `workflow`). Cannot be changed after creation.
- `runtime`: `docker` (build from Dockerfile), `image` (prebuilt), `static`, or native `node`/`python`/`ruby`/`go`/`elixir`/`rust`. No `php` native runtime, so Docker is required.
- `plan`: e.g. `free`. If omitted, a new web service gets `0.5c-512mb` (paid), so `plan: free` must be explicit. `free` = 0.1 CPU / 512 MB.
- `region`: `oregon` (default), `ohio`, `virginia`, `frankfurt`, `singapore`. Cannot be changed after creation.
- `rootDir`: service root within the repo; changes outside it do not trigger a build. Default repo root.
- `dockerfilePath`: relative to the repo root; default `./Dockerfile`.
- `dockerContext`: relative to the repo root; default the repo root.
- `dockerCommand`: overrides the Dockerfile `CMD`.
- `healthCheckPath`: web services only, starts with `/`.
- `autoDeploy`: deprecated. Use `autoDeployTrigger`: `commit` (default for new services), `checksPass`, `off`. If both present, `autoDeployTrigger` wins.
- `buildFilter`: glob paths to include/ignore (sub-key names not visible in the fetched text).
- `envVars`: list of entries. Confirmed in prose: `sync: false` ("Render prompts you for these values only during the initial Blueprint creation", ignored on later syncs, not copied to preview environments); `generateValue: true` -> if the variable does not exist Render sets "a randomized, base64-encoded, 256-bit value (looks like this: `B0jrphAPOY7pg92AN0c9MN4yecczLMdwnx4OkA1KFUk=`)"; `fromDatabase` and `fromService` references. The `key:` / `value:` example lines themselves were not visible; they are the long-standing syntax but unverified today.
  - Note for Laravel: the generated value is bare base64 of 32 bytes with no `base64:` prefix, so it cannot be used directly as `APP_KEY` unless the app or entrypoint adds the prefix. (Inference from the documented format, not a Render statement.)
- File location: `render.yaml` at the repo root by default; a custom path can be given in the "Blueprint Path" field — https://render.com/docs/infrastructure-as-code
- Interplay of `rootDir` with `dockerfilePath`/`dockerContext` (both documented as relative to the repo root) is not spelled out; unconfirmed whether they become relative to `rootDir` when it is set.

**Blueprint on Hobby with no payment method**: unconfirmed. https://render.com/docs/infrastructure-as-code states no plan or payment requirement. https://render.com/docs/your-first-deploy says "This tutorial uses free Render resources. No payment is required." (dashboard flow, not Blueprint). An old community thread (snippet only) says a payment prompt during Blueprint setup means some resource in the file is on a paid plan. Practical rule: every resource must say `plan: free`.

**preDeployCommand**: not available on free. "The pre-deploy command is available for paid web services, private services, and background workers." (https://render.com/docs/deploys; same restriction in the Blueprint spec.) One-off jobs and shell access are also unavailable on free (https://render.com/docs/free).

**Documented migration route for free**: https://render.com/docs/deploy-rails-8 says "The pre-deploy command requires a paid compute plan." and "If you're starting on the Free compute plan, you can keep database migration logic in your build script." For Docker, the official Laravel guide (https://render.com/docs/deploy-php-laravel-docker) says to "add a deploy script that will be run when your PHP app starts up"; the script contents were not visible, so that it runs migrations is unconfirmed. Practical consequence for a Docker service: run `artisan migrate --force` in the container start script/entrypoint (the Docker build is a poor place, since it would need database credentials as build args).

---

### 3. Render free-plan details affecting a PHP app

- **Outbound to external Postgres**: only ports 25, 465, 587 are blocked on free ("Free web services can't send outbound network traffic on ports 25, 465, or 587"). Port 5432 is not mentioned or blocked. One caveat on the same page: Render may suspend a free web service with an unusually high volume of service-initiated traffic, and the fetch summary said "Accessing an external database" is listed as an example of such traffic. Not an issue at demo scale, but worth knowing — https://render.com/docs/free
- **Regions**: Oregon, Ohio, Virginia, Frankfurt (Germany), Singapore; region cannot be changed after creation — https://render.com/docs/regions. Whether all are open to Free is not stated (no restriction documented).
- **Environment variables at build time**: yes, both. "If you set environment variables for a Docker-based service, Render automatically translates those values to Docker build arguments that are available during your image's build process", and they are available at runtime as normal env vars. Render warns not to reference sensitive build args in the Dockerfile; use secret files instead (copied to the root of the build context at build; at runtime under `/etc/secrets/<filename>`) — https://render.com/docs/docker, https://render.com/docs/configure-environment-variables. Whether the Dockerfile must declare `ARG` is not stated (standard Docker behaviour says yes).
- Changing an env var offers "Save, rebuild, and deploy", "Save and deploy", or "Save only" — https://render.com/docs/configure-environment-variables
- **Logs**: runtime logs on the service's Logs page in the Dashboard (searchable); per-deploy build logs on the service's Deploys page. Retention on Hobby: 7 days — https://render.com/docs/logging. Log streams are supported on free (https://render.com/docs/free).

---

### 4. Neon connection details for PHP (PDO pgsql / libpq)

- **Parameters**: the docs' `.env` form is `PGHOST`, `PGDATABASE`, `PGUSER`, `PGPASSWORD`, `PGPORT=5432` — https://neon.com/faqs/find-connection-details-neon-console, https://neon.com/docs/guides/django
- **Hostname naming**: direct `ep-cool-darkness-a1b2c3d4.us-east-2.aws.neon.tech`; pooled `ep-cool-darkness-a1b2c3d4-pooler.us-east-2.aws.neon.tech`. Only the `-pooler` suffix on the compute ID differs — https://neon.com/faqs/find-connection-details-neon-console
- **SSL modes**: SSL/TLS is mandatory. Console-generated strings end with `sslmode=require&channel_binding=require` (https://neon.com/docs/connect/connect-from-any-app). "Neon recommends that you always use `verify-full` mode." — https://neon.com/docs/connect/connect-securely
- **CA**: "Neon uses the public ISRG Root X1 certificate" from Let's Encrypt. Root bundle paths: Debian/Ubuntu `/etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt`, Alpine `/etc/ssl/cert.pem`, macOS `/etc/ssl/cert.pem` — same page. (In a slim Docker image the `ca-certificates` package must be installed for these to exist; my note, not Neon's.)
- **`sslrootcert=system`**: not mentioned in Neon's current docs, which instead say to point `sslrootcert`/`PGSSLROOTCERT` at the OS bundle file. It is a libpq 16 feature: PostgreSQL 16 release notes, "Allow libpq to use the system certificate pool for certificate verification ... enabled with `sslrootcert=system`, which also enables `sslmode=verify-full`" (https://www.postgresql.org/docs/release/16.0/); and "any weaker setting will result in an error" (https://www.postgresql.org/docs/16/libpq-connect.html). A Neon blog post (search snippet only, not docs) shows it working against Neon with a psql 16 client. Portable choice for any libpq version: `sslmode=verify-full` plus `sslrootcert=<bundle path>`.
- **`channel_binding=require`**: optional; uses SCRAM-SHA-256-PLUS; "clients built on libpq ... need libpq 13 or later"; if a tool errors, drop it and keep `sslmode=verify-full` — https://neon.com/docs/connect/connect-securely. Whether Laravel's pgsql config passes this parameter through is not documented by Neon (unconfirmed).
- **SNI / "The endpoint ID is not specified"**: caused by clients without TLS SNI. "SNI support was added to `libpq` ... in Postgres 14"; clients on libpq >= 14 work. Workarounds in order of preference: (A) `options=endpoint%3D[endpoint_id]` in the connection string; (B) key/value form `dbname=neondb options=endpoint=[endpoint_id]`; (D) password field `endpoint=<endpoint_id>;<password>` or with `$` as separator — this downgrades authentication to plain password and Neon intends to deprecate it — https://neon.com/docs/connect/connection-errors. The endpoint ID is the `ep-...` part of the hostname. No PHP-specific notes on that page; the Laravel guide repeats A and D (https://neon.com/docs/guides/laravel).
- **Pooler startup parameters**: through the `-pooler` host only `client_encoding`, `datestyle`, `timezone`, `standard_conforming_strings`, `application_name` are accepted; anything else gives "unsupported startup parameter" — https://neon.com/docs/connect/connection-errors. Relevant if the app sets `search_path` or other options at connect time, and it means workaround A (`options=endpoint...`) may not combine with the pooled host (unconfirmed; use the direct host if SNI workaround is needed).
- **Default Postgres version**: 18. "Postgres 18 is now the default for newly created Neon projects." — https://neon.com/docs/changelog/2026-06-05. Supported: 14-18 (https://neon.com/docs/postgresql/postgres-version-policy).
- **AWS regions**: `aws-us-east-1` N. Virginia, `aws-us-east-2` Ohio, `aws-us-west-2` Oregon, `aws-eu-central-1` Frankfurt, `aws-eu-west-2` London, `aws-ap-southeast-1` Singapore, `aws-ap-southeast-2` Sydney, `aws-sa-east-1` São Paulo. Europe: Frankfurt and London. Region is fixed at creation — https://neon.com/docs/introduction/regions. Frankfurt exists on both Render and Neon, so the pair can be co-located.
- **pg_trgm**: `CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pg_trgm;` run from the SQL Editor or psql (or a migration); GIN/GiST examples use `gin_trgm_ops` / `gist_trgm_ops` — https://neon.com/docs/extensions/pg_trgm. No privilege note; version 1.6.

---

### 5. Neon console steps as documented

Create a project — https://neon.com/docs/manage/projects
1. Open the Neon Console (console.neon.tech).
2. "Click **New Project**."
3. "Enter a **Project name** and choose a **Region** (the cloud provider is part of the region)."
4. Under **Services**, "**Postgres database** is on by default (expand it to set the **Postgres version**)."
5. "Click **Create project**."

What a new project gets (same page, plus https://neon.com/docs/get-started/signing-up): root branch named `production` when created in the Console (`main` via API/CLI); database `neondb` unless another name is given; role `neondb_owner`; one primary read-write compute. Still current.

Find connection parameters — https://neon.com/docs/connect/connect-from-any-app, https://neon.com/faqs/find-connection-details-neon-console
1. Click **Connect** "in the Console nav".
2. The **Connect to your branch** modal opens.
3. "Select a **Branch**, **Compute**, **Database**, and **Role**."
4. The **Connection pooling** toggle is on by default (pooled string with `-pooler`); turn it off for the direct string.
- SQL Editor: sidebar **Postgres database** > **SQL Editor**.
- Unconfirmed: a "Parameters only" selector. It appeared in a search snippet but not on any page actually fetched; the FAQ only shows a section headed "Individual fields for a `.env` file". If the option is missing in the UI, the parameters can be read out of the connection string.

---

### Contradictions / corrections to my earlier report

1. Modal name: Neon's docs call it "Connect to your branch", not "Connect to your database" (I did not name it earlier, but any older reference to the latter is out of date).
2. Earlier I suggested running migrations with Neon's direct connection as a separate step. On a free Render service there is no pre-deploy command, one-off job, or shell, so migrations must run in the container start script (or from a local machine). The runtime could therefore need the direct (non-pooled) host for the migrate step, or simply use the direct host for everything; at 105 usable connections on 0.25 CU, the direct host is adequate for a demo and avoids the pooler's startup-parameter and SET limitations.
3. Earlier I listed the SNI password workaround (`endpoint=<id>$<password>`) alongside the `options=endpoint` one as equivalent. The connection-errors page says the password form downgrades authentication and is slated for deprecation; prefer `options=endpoint%3D<id>`, or better, a libpq >= 14 in the image so no workaround is needed.
4. Earlier I said Neon's 90-day inactivity deletion wording was ambiguous in scope. A second read of the regions page was summarised as applying to Free projects in the deprecated Azure regions; still not stated outright, still irrelevant for an AWS-region project.
5. Nothing found today contradicts the earlier Render figures (15 min spin-down, about one minute cold start, 750 hours, 5 GB bandwidth, 500 build minutes, Postgres 30-day expiry).
```

### Backend security and integrity review

**Prompt written by the assistant**

```text
Read-only release review of a Laravel 13 REST API (PHP 8.4, PostgreSQL 18, Sanctum SPA session cookies) at /Users/[redacted: account name]/Desktop/eneba-marketplace/api. It is an internship assignment: an Eneba-style marketplace for digital game keys with a simulated payment. DO NOT modify, create or delete any file, do not run git commands that change anything, do not start containers, and do not print values from any `.env` file (do not read `.env` files at all; `.env.example` is fine). PHP is not installed on the host, so do not try to run tests; this is a reading review.

Goal: find real defects in security, authorization, data integrity and recovery edge cases before submission. I want an independent read, so I am deliberately not telling you what I believe is correct. Report only issues you can point to in code with file:line, and for each say: severity (critical / high / medium / low), the concrete scenario (request sequence or state → wrong outcome), and the smallest fix. Explicitly say "checked, no issue" for areas you verified so I know coverage. Do not suggest refactors or new features, and do not report style.

Read first: docs/ARCHITECTURE.md (sections on cart/checkout, states, recovery, auth) for the intended design, then the code:
- routes/api.php, routes/web.php, routes/console.php, bootstrap/app.php, config/{cors,sanctum,session,auth,checkout}.php, app/Providers/AppServiceProvider.php
- app/Http/Controllers/*, app/Http/Requests/**, app/Http/Resources/*, app/Http/Middleware/*
- app/Services/Checkout/* (PlaceOrder, SettleOrder, QuoteCart, InventoryAllocator, SimulatedPaymentGateway), app/Services/Homepage/*, app/Support/*
- app/Models/*, app/Enums/*, database/migrations/*, database/seeders/*
- tests/Feature/** only to see what is and is not covered

Specific questions to answer:
1. Authorization: can any route return or change another user's order, payment or license key? Is a license key value ever serialized outside a paid order's detail response for its buyer (check every Resource, model `$hidden`, `toArray`, error responses, logs, exception messages)? Any mass-assignment exposure (`$fillable`/`$guarded`) reachable from request input?
2. Auth: registration/login/logout validation, throttling, session regeneration on login, session invalidation on logout, CSRF coverage of every state-changing route, cookie flags and CORS/stateful-domain configuration in production, user enumeration, password rules.
3. Checkout integrity: trace reserve → pay → settle for races. Two buyers and the last key; the same buyer double-submitting; pay racing with expiry; pay racing with cancel; reconcile racing with pay; a crash between the gateway decision and the database commit; an order whose `expires_at` passed while a pay request is in flight; prices changing between quote and order; integer overflow or negative quantities/prices; currency. Are totals always recomputed server-side? Is every state transition guarded under a row lock and by a database constraint?
4. Idempotency key handling: scope per user, payload mismatch, replay after the order ended.
5. Input validation on list/search endpoints: SQL injection through raw expressions (`orderByRaw`, `whereRaw`, `selectRaw`, `DB::raw`), unbounded `per_page`/page/array sizes, ReDoS or expensive trigram queries, rate limits that are missing or keyed on a spoofable header.
6. The request-time housekeeping middleware (ReleaseExpiredReservations): can an anonymous visitor make it do unbounded work, or can a failure inside it break public pages?
7. Homepage resolvers: can stored configuration cause an open redirect, an exception that takes down the whole response, or leak unpublished/sold-out data?
8. Error handling: do production error responses leak stack traces, SQL, or internal ids? Sequential integer ids exposed where a UUID should be?
9. Migrations and seeders: anything destructive on redeploy; `down()` correctness is low priority.
10. Test gaps: list the 5 most valuable missing tests, only for behaviour that is security- or integrity-relevant.

Final output: a severity-ordered findings list (keep it tight: under ~900 words), then the "checked, no issue" list, then the missing-test list. Mark each finding as CONFIRMED (you traced it fully) or PLAUSIBLE (needs a run to confirm).
```

**Report returned**

```text
Read-only review complete. Nothing was run, modified or created, and no `.env` file was read. No critical or high findings; two medium, the rest low. CONFIRMED means traced fully in code; PLAUSIBLE means it needs a run.

All paths are under `/Users/[redacted: account name]/Desktop/eneba-marketplace/api/`.

## Findings (severity order)

**1. MEDIUM, CONFIRMED: every seeded license key can be computed from the public repo.**
- Where: `database/seeders/CatalogSeeder.php:182` and `:213-216`.
- Scenario: the code is `sha256("key|{product-slug}|{seller-slug}|{n}")[:15]`. Product slugs come from `GET /api/products`, seller slugs from the product page, and n is 1 to 12. Anyone can list every key in production without an account or payment, which bypasses "key codes never leave the checkout".
- Fix: `hash_hmac('sha256', ..., config('app.key'))` in `keyCode()`. On an already-seeded database this inserts a second set of keys once, so reseed, or state in the docs that seed keys are placeholders.

**2. MEDIUM, CONFIRMED: "one checkout in progress per buyer" is not enforced under concurrency, so one free account can hold the whole stock.**
- Where: `app/Services/Checkout/PlaceOrder.php:56-57` reads the buyer's other pending orders with no lock, then inserts at `:73`. No constraint backs the rule: `2026_11_18_000001_make_checkout_states_explicit.php:43` is a non-unique index.
- Scenario: one buyer sends N parallel `POST /api/orders` with different Idempotency-Keys and different offers. None sees the others, so N pending orders exist, each holding up to 20 lines × 10 keys (`config/checkout.php:8-9`), within the 30/min throttle (`AppServiceProvider.php:63-65`). Repeated every 10 minutes, the store stays sold out at zero cost.
- Even without the race, about ten accounts achieve the same sequentially; that part is a design limit to document.
- Fix: `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX ... ON orders (user_id) WHERE status = 'pending'`, and on that violation re-run the cancel-then-create once.

**3. LOW, PLAUSIBLE: deadlock between two concurrent payments returns 500 after the charge.**
- Where: `app/Services/Checkout/SettleOrder.php:192-198`, inside the transaction at `:155`.
- Scenario: `countSales` locks product and seller rows in order-line order. Order A (seller X then Y) and order B (seller Y then X) paid together deadlock; PostgreSQL aborts one and `pay` returns 500 although the gateway charged. It self-heals on the next reconcile.
- Fix: increment in ascending product id, then seller id, or pass `attempts: 3` to `DB::transaction` in `apply()` (it re-reads under the lock).

**4. LOW, CONFIRMED: any `Authorization: Bearer x` header gives a 500.**
- Where: every route that resolves the sanctum guard: `routes/api.php:51,72,74`, `CartController.php:21`, `AuthController.php:61`.
- Scenario: with no session user, Sanctum's guard (`vendor/laravel/sanctum/src/Guard.php:40-43`) queries `personal_access_tokens`. No migration creates that table; Sanctum 4.4 only publishes it. `GET /api/auth/me` with a Bearer header fails with a logged QueryException.
- Fix: `Sanctum::getAccessTokenFromRequestUsing(fn () => '');` in `AppServiceProvider::boot`.

**5. LOW, CONFIRMED: renaming a game, platform or region in the data files makes every later container start fail.**
- Where: `CatalogSeeder.php:92` looks products up by a slug derived from the name.
- Scenario: a changed title gives a new slug, so the seeder inserts a second row for the same (game, platform, region). That violates the unique index (`2026_11_17_000001_split_games_from_products.php:56`); the seeder throws and `docker/entrypoint.sh:47` (under `set -e`) stops the container.
- Fix: `updateOrCreate(['game_id' => ..., 'platform_id' => ..., 'region_id' => ...], ['slug' => ..., 'name' => ...])`.

**6. LOW: crafted input gives 500 instead of 422/404 (no leak with debug off).**
- CONFIRMED: `AppServiceProvider.php:70`. `email` sent as an array makes `(string)` raise "Array to string conversion" inside the rate limiter. Fix: use the value only if `is_string`.
- PLAUSIBLE: `ListProductsRequest.php:60-61` with `ProductController.php:45-46`. `min_price=3000000000` passes `integer` but overflows the int4 comparison. Fix: `max:2147483647`.
- PLAUSIBLE: `ProductController.php:90` and `ListProductsRequest.php:55,57`. Invalid UTF-8 (`/api/products/%FF`, `platform=%FF`) reaches PostgreSQL as an invalid byte sequence. Fix: constrain slugs to `[a-z0-9-]+`.

**7. LOW, CONFIRMED: the catalog routes have no rate limit.**
- Where: `routes/api.php:31,41,44-45`.
- Scenario: `/api/products?q=` runs the same trigram search that suggestions throttle. `page` is unbounded (`ListProductsRequest.php:63`), `type[]` has no `max` (`:58`), and platform/region run up to 40 `exists` queries per request. Production has 8 Apache workers.
- Fix: reuse `throttle:search` on these routes and add `max:20` to `type`.

**8. LOW or informational, CONFIRMED.**
- `RegisterRequest.php:15`: the `unique` rule reveals which emails are registered.
- `RegisterRequest.php:17`: `max:255` accepts passwords that bcrypt silently truncates at 72 bytes.
- `AppServiceProvider.php:69-70`: the 60/min edge bucket is shared by all visitors behind the frontend, and the 10/min email bucket lets anyone lock a known email out of sign-in.
- `QuoteCart.php:132-145`: the quote returns name, seller and price of switched-off offers for any offer id (intentional per the test, but it is exposure).

## Checked, no issue

- **Order authorization:** every order route looks up through `$request->user()->orders()` by UUID (`OrderController.php:105-109`); no route-model binding.
- **Key exposure:** keys are serialized only at `OrderResource.php:33-39` (paid order, sold keys, the buyer); `LicenseKey` hides `code`; no key appears in quotes, 409/402 bodies, history, homepage, exception messages or `report()` calls.
- **Mass assignment:** the only request-to-model path is `User::create($request->validated())`, limited to name, email and password.
- **Auth:** the session is regenerated on login and register and invalidated on logout; login gives one message for both failures; the throttle is on login and register.
- **CSRF and cookies:** every POST sits behind the stateful pipeline; cross-site requests get no session; the cookie is HttpOnly, Lax, host-only, and Secure is enforced by the entrypoint; CORS allows one origin without credentials; only `X-Forwarded-Proto` is trusted; diagnostics are off.
- **Checkout races:** I found no path to a double sale, double charge or release-while-chargeable for last key, double submit, pay vs expiry, pay vs cancel, reconcile vs pay, crash after charge, or deadline passing mid-pay.
- **Checkout inputs:** totals are always recomputed server-side; the shown price is required and compared; quantities and prices are validated and constrained in the database; currency is fixed to EUR.
- **Idempotency:** scoped per user, cart-hash mismatch gives 422, and a replay after the order ended returns that order (the frontend rotates the key, `CartPage.tsx:40-46`).
- **SQL injection:** all raw expressions are constants or bound; LIKE is escaped; `per_page` is capped at 48; no ReDoS.
- **Housekeeping middleware:** at most 20 orders per request, each isolated by try/catch.
- **Homepage:** `InternalPath` blocks external, `//`, backslash and control-character links; resolver exceptions are isolated; only purchasable products and active banners are returned; odd config shapes are safe.
- **Errors and ids:** JSON only, generic 500 in production; orders use a UUID; sequential ids are exposed only for offers, blocks and the user's own id.
- **Redeploy:** migrations are additive, the entrypoint runs `migrate --force` only, and the seeder never touches existing keys or sales counts.

## Five most valuable missing tests

1. A request with `Authorization: Bearer x` to `/api/auth/me`, `/api/cart/quote` and `/api/orders` never returns 500.
2. One buyer starting parallel checkouts with different keys and different offers ends with at most one pending order. The existing test uses a single key, so it passes by stock exhaustion.
3. Two multi-line orders with sellers in crossing order paid in parallel both end paid with no 500.
4. `pay` racing the expiry sweep at the deadline ends as exactly one of paid or expired with consistent keys; plus an idempotent replay after the order expired, failed or was cancelled holds nothing.
5. CSRF is actually enforced: a POST from the frontend origin without a token or `Sec-Fetch-Site` gets 419. The middleware skips itself under PHPUnit (`PreventRequestForgery.php:99`) and `verify-deployment.mjs` has no 419 check, so nothing proves it today.
```

Verify from official/primary sources rather than memory; give the source URL for each fact. Do not include any personal or project names in your web requests.

Questions:

1. Laravel Sanctum, current official docs (laravel.com/docs, latest version — also tell me what the current stable Laravel major version is and whether `php artisan install:api` is still the way to add Sanctum and routes/api.php):
